# BlokeBot plugin author reference v1

This reference is generated from the canonical plugin contracts. Regenerate the author artifacts instead of editing its contract tables.

## Runtime contract

| Contract | Version |
| --- | ---: |
| Manifest | 1 |
| Package policy | 1 |
| Typed value | 1 |
| Host API | 1 |
| Lua | 5.4 |

Plugins are `FullyTrusted` and run with `BlokeBotAccount` operating-system access. The `AvailabilityOnly` process boundary limits availability failures; it is not a security boundary. The Lua standard library is `Full`. Do not describe a plugin as sandboxed.

## Identity and submission

Use one stable lowercase plugin ID. `PluginIdentifierSyntaxContract.Current` requires 1-64 characters, a lowercase ASCII letter prefix, a lowercase ASCII letter or digit suffix, and separators from `.-_`. Adjacent separators are rejected.

A marketplace submission names the manifest's declared semantic version and one mutable Git tag. `PluginGitTagSyntaxContract.Current` accepts 1-128 characters and rejects all-hex values 7-64 characters long, so a commit SHA is never plugin identity.

The curated repository path is `plugins/<plugin-id>/plugin.toml`. The directory name exactly matches the manifest ID. The manifest owns author, search tags, optional presentation URLs, supported release targets, release identity, compatibility, and runtime declarations; there is no global catalogue or generated index.

## Package and targets

The package root contains `plugin.toml`. Declare every Lua module, browser or media asset, and other payload. Paths are relative canonical `/` paths. The canonical package boundary reports concrete `PluginPackageEntryErrorCode` values for absolute, dot-segment, link, undeclared, missing, oversized, duplicate, and case-folded-collision failures. Limits are 512 entries and 67108864 bytes.

Every asset and payload declares its path, purpose, maximum size, and supported runtime identifiers. Every declaration target must be one of the manifest's supported release targets; separate declarations may select different target subsets. Payloads may be native files, .NET assemblies, WebAssembly, or another plugin-managed type. Admission enforces declarations, targets, paths, links, collisions, and byte limits; it does not infer or police a payload's byte type from its extension.

Supported runtime identifiers:

- `linux-x64`
- `linux-arm64`
- `osx-arm64`
- `win-x64`
- `win-arm64`

Lua modules are the only BlokeBot-managed entrypoints. Other payloads and their dependencies remain the trusted plugin's responsibility.

## Typed Lua API

Import the versioned public module once with `local blokebot = require("blokebot")`. Each host operation is a named function with generated LuaLS parameter, return, structured-value, failure, and cancellation types. The generic module/operation dispatcher is private worker transport.

`context.current` takes no arguments. The host derives its result from the admitted invocation; plugins cannot supply or override identities. Every result includes `kind`, `pluginId`, `pluginVersion`, and `pluginTag`. Channel results also include `hostId`, `featureId`, and any applicable `actor`, `stream`, `command`, `event`, `schedule`, or `web` map. Automation and page results include their exact host, feature, and invocation map. Migration results include only the migration identity in addition to the common fields.

`settings.installation` and `settings.feature` also take no arguments. They return only configured values declared for the invoking plugin and exact installation or host-feature owner. Protected values are available only inside that admitted plugin invocation. Do not copy protected values into logs, diagnostics, audit fields, failure messages, or generated documents. Missing optional values are absent from the returned map; a configuration read or decryption fault returns only the generic typed host failure. Settings reads are unavailable during migration.

| Function | Description | Contexts | Parameters | Returns |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `blokebot.context.current` | Returns the admitted invocation context. Plugin and feature identities cannot be supplied by the caller. | installation, channel, automation, migration, page |  | `BlokeBotContext` |
| `blokebot.settings.installation` | Returns configured installation settings declared by this plugin. | installation, channel, automation, page |  | `BlokeBotInstallationSettings` |
| `blokebot.settings.feature` | Returns configured settings for the admitted host feature. | channel, automation, page |  | `BlokeBotFeatureSettings` |
| `blokebot.diagnostics.log` | Writes a redaction-safe plugin diagnostic. | installation, channel, automation, migration, page | `level: BlokeBotDiagnosticLevel`, `message: string` | `nil` |
| `blokebot.responses.chat` | Replies in the admitted channel. | channel | `message: string` | `nil` |
| `blokebot.responses.whisper` | Replies privately to the admitted actor. | channel | `message: string` | `nil` |
| `blokebot.chat.send` | Sends a message to the admitted channel. | channel | `message: string` | `nil` |
| `blokebot.overlay.play_cue` | Admits an overlay cue for the current channel. | channel | `target_id: BlokeBotOverlayTargetId`, `cue_id: BlokeBotOverlayCueId` | `nil` |
| `blokebot.points.add` | Adds a non-negative point amount to a viewer balance. | channel | `viewer: string`, `amount: BlokeBotPointAmount`, `reason: string` | `BlokeBotPointBalance` |
| `blokebot.twitch.create_marker` | Creates a Twitch stream marker for the admitted channel. | channel | `description: string` | `nil` |
| `blokebot.schedules.once` | Creates a one-time feature schedule. | channel | `handler_id: string`, `due_at: string`, `input: BlokeBotScheduleInput` | `BlokeBotScheduleId` |
| `blokebot.schedules.recurring` | Creates a recurring feature schedule. | channel | `handler_id: string`, `due_at: string`, `interval_seconds: integer`, `input: BlokeBotScheduleInput` | `BlokeBotScheduleId` |
| `blokebot.schedules.cancel` | Cancels a schedule owned by the current feature generation. | channel | `schedule_id: BlokeBotScheduleId` | `nil` |
| `blokebot.storage.execute` | Executes one supported statement against plugin-private SQLite. | installation, channel, automation, migration, page | `sql: string`, `parameters: BlokeBotSqlParameters` | `number` |
| `blokebot.storage.query` | Queries plugin-private SQLite. | installation, channel, automation, migration, page | `sql: string`, `parameters: BlokeBotSqlParameters` | `BlokeBotSqlRow[]` |
| `blokebot.http.send` | Sends a bounded outbound HTTP request through the host policy. | installation, channel, automation, page | `request: BlokeBotHttpRequest` | `BlokeBotHttpOutcome` |

A successful named call returns the documented value directly. `pcall` receives a `BlokeBotHostFailure` table when the host rejects a call, including its stable `kind`, `code`, and safe message. An uncaught failure terminates the invocation. Cancellation remains authoritative and ends the invocation with a `BlokeBotHostCancellation`; it never becomes an ambiguous nil return.

Declare every host module your plugin uses with a minimum and maximum API version. Treat safe failure messages as operator-readable detail, not provider internals.

## Invocations and effects

Features may declare settings, Twitch requirements, commands, event handlers, schedules, webhooks, actions, automation definitions and templates, generated pages, and embedded pages. A declaration does not register a live feature or run Lua.

Host-call waits return the canonical `Cancelled` outcome with a `PluginCancellationReason` and whether the worker terminated. Cancellation stops the coroutine from waiting and a later result is not resumed. It does not promise to undo a chat message, schedule, HTTP request, SQLite write, or other effect that completed before cancellation won.

Use the `storage` module for plugin-private SQLite operations and `http.send` for approved web integrations. Generated and embedded UI pages must use declared modules or browser assets. A failed update migration becomes `MigrationFailed` and leaves the selected update `Faulted` without resuming the old generation. A worker crash reports `WorkerExited` and is isolated by the `AvailabilityOnly` boundary.

## Executable examples

Published sources live under `examples/plugins`. Optional package-local `tests.toml` files define author-harness scenarios; they are not part of normal plugin package validation. The Contracts test harness packages each example from local files, validates it for every supported RID, and executes every declared scenario through the supported worker protocol on the current RID. The harness has deterministic host adapters and does not install into the production local-source directory, join production inventory, contact Twitch, or make third-party network requests.

## Remove

`Remove` is destructive for plugin-owned state. Canonical removal owners delete the installed package, installation and channel settings, feature state, configuration, secrets, schedules, private data, automation definitions, ledgers, dependent flows and nodes, run history, marketplace receipts, and invocation context. A retained owner resource faults removal as `RemovalFailed`; there is no purge or retention mode. The derived marketplace snapshot is not plugin-owned installation state and remains discoverable for a later install.
